I mean, I was definitely more irritable and snapped at people, like I said, but I was still getting my work done. Like I said, I wasn't keeping track. It might have been a few days, maybe a week... I just know it was a stretch of time where I felt that way, but I don't have a clear timeline in my head.\n\nDoctor: During that stretch when you felt unusually energetic and irritable, did you notice you needed a lot less sleep than usual but still felt rested?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't notice anything like that. I mean, I work with my hands all day, so it takes a lot out of me... I was still sleeping my regular hours, as far as I can remember. I definitely wasn't running on just a few hours of sleep, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you felt unusually energetic, did you also notice your thoughts racing faster than usual, or find yourself taking on a lot more projects or activities than you normally would?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely took on a lot more. I mean, I started picking up extra restoration jobs on top of my regular work. And at the shop, I was... well, I was mentoring the younger carpenters a lot harder than I usually do. Just really on them. I even sat down and drew up these new business plans, and I found myself reaching out to people I normally would have waited weeks to call. So, yeah, I was definitely doing a lot more than normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any specific event or workday during that energetic stretch—like a particular job you finished or a weekend—that might help you estimate whether it lasted at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I've tried to think back on it, but... no, not really. When I get going like that, the days just sort of blend together. I mean, I know I was in the shop working on those extra jobs, but I couldn't point to a specific Tuesday or a weekend and say, 'Yeah, that's when it started and ended.' \n\nIt was just a continuous stretch for a bit. I just wasn't paying attention to the calendar like that.\n\nDoctor: During that energetic stretch, did you feel unusually confident or capable—like you could do things better or more easily than usual, or that your ideas or abilities were especially great?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I figured I could handle my wife's care and take on every single job at the same time without dropping a thing. I just felt completely capable of doing it all, no problem.\n\nDoctor: Did your wife or anyone on your crew ever comment that this burst of energy and confidence seemed to go on for about a week or more, rather than just a couple of days?\n\nPatient: No, nobody ever said anything like that. My wife hasn't really been in a position to notice... and the guys at the shop, they just knew I was on them about getting things done. If they thought it lasted a week or more, they didn't mention it to me. They just kind of kept their heads down and worked. So, I really don't know how long it seemed to them.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me approximately how long that energetic stretch lasted in relation to something memorable—like was it shorter than a weekend trip, or did it stretch across more than one full week, such as spanning from one weekend to the next?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. Like I said, I wasn't really tracking the days. It wasn't tied to a trip or a specific weekend or anything like that. It was just a block of time where I was going full tilt, but whether it was a few days or a full week... I really couldn't tell you for sure. The days just kind of ran together. Our whole daily routine changed, and... yeah, that's really when this whole shift in my mood and behavior started.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days or more where you felt unusually energetic, elated, or needed much less sleep than usual, even if it wasn't connected to anything happening around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. Right at the beginning of all this, I did have stretches where I felt just... really fired up. Like I was almost unstoppable. \n\nBut it wasn't exactly a great thing, because if my wife or one of the guys on my crew even questioned me about something, I would just snap at them. Over absolutely nothing. So yeah, I definitely had periods like what you're describing.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you felt unusually energetic and 'fired up,' how many days in a row did that last?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure, to be honest. I didn't really keep track of the days. It was just... a stretch of time where I felt like that, but I couldn't give you a specific number.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you felt unusually energetic and irritable, did it cause serious problems—like getting into major conflicts at work, being unable to complete jobs, or anyone suggesting you needed help—or were you still able to manage your day-to-day responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, it didn't cause any major problems like that. I mean, I was definitely more irritable and snapped at people, like I said, but I was still getting my work done. I didn't get into any huge fights that threatened my job, and nobody told me I needed to go to a hospital or anything. I just kept working through it, even if I was a bit harder to be around.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back, would you say that period of feeling unusually energetic and irritable lasted more like a few days, about a week, or longer than that?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't say for sure. Like I said, I wasn't keeping track. It might have been a few days, maybe a week... I just know it was a stretch of time where I felt that way, but I don't have a clear timeline in my head.\n\nDoctor: During that stretch when you felt unusually energetic and irritable, did you notice you needed a lot less sleep than usual but still felt rested?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't notice anything like that. I mean, I work with my hands all day, so it takes a lot out of me... I was still sleeping my regular hours, as far as I can remember. I definitely wasn't running on just a few hours of sleep, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you felt unusually energetic, did you also notice your thoughts racing faster than usual, or find yourself taking on a lot more projects or activities than you normally would?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely took on a lot more. I mean, I started picking up extra restoration jobs on top of my regular work. And at the shop, I was... well, I was mentoring the younger carpenters a lot harder than I usually do. Just really on them. I even sat down and drew up these new business plans, and I found myself reaching out to people I normally would have waited weeks to call. So, yeah, I was definitely doing a lot more than normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any specific event or workday during that energetic stretch—like a particular job you finished or a weekend—that might help you estimate whether it lasted at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I've tried to think back on it, but... no, not really. When I get going like that, the days just sort of blend together. I mean, I know I was in the shop working on those extra jobs, but I couldn't point to a specific Tuesday or a weekend and say, 'Yeah, that's when it started and ended.' \n\nIt was just a continuous stretch for a bit. I couldn't swear to you if it was exactly four days in a row or not. I just wasn't paying attention to the calendar like that.\n\nDoctor: During that energetic stretch, did you feel unusually confident or capable—like you could do things better or more easily than usual, or that your ideas or abilities were especially great?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. It was just... a stretch of time where I felt like that, but I couldn't give you a specific number.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you felt unusually energetic and irritable, did it cause serious problems—like getting into major conflicts at work, being unable to complete jobs, or anyone suggesting you needed help—or were you still able to manage your day-to-day responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, it didn't cause any major problems like that. I mean, I was definitely more irritable and snapped at people, like I said, but I was still getting my work done. I didn't get into any huge fights that threatened my job, and nobody told me I needed to go to a hospital or anything. I just kept working through it, even if I was a bit harder to be around.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back, would you say that period of feeling unusually energetic and irritable lasted more like a few days, about a week, or longer than that?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't say for sure. Like I said, I wasn't keeping track. It might have been a few days, maybe a week... I just know it was a stretch of time where I felt that way, but I don't have a clear timeline in my head.\n\nDoctor: During that stretch when you felt unusually energetic and irritable, did you notice you needed a lot less sleep than usual but still felt rested?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't notice anything like that. I mean, I work with my hands all day, so it takes a lot out of me... I was still sleeping my regular hours, as far as I can remember. I definitely wasn't running on just a few hours of sleep, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you felt unusually energetic, did you also notice your thoughts racing faster than usual, or find yourself taking on a lot more projects or activities than you normally would?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely took on a lot more. I mean, I started picking up extra restoration jobs on top of my regular work. And at the shop, I was... well, I was mentoring the younger carpenters a lot harder than I usually do. Just really on them. I even sat down and drew up these new business plans, and I found myself reaching out to people I normally would have waited weeks to call. So, yeah, I was definitely doing a lot more than normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any specific event or workday during that energetic stretch—like a particular job you finished or a weekend—that might help you estimate whether it lasted at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I've tried to think back on it, but... no, not really. When I get going like that, the days just sort of blend together. I mean, I know I was in the shop working on those extra jobs, but I couldn't point to a specific Tuesday or a weekend and say, 'Yeah, that's when it started and ended.' \n\nIt was just a continuous stretch for a bit. I couldn't swear to you if it was exactly four days in a row or not. I just wasn't paying attention to the calendar like that.\n\nDoctor: During that energetic stretch, did you feel unusually confident or capable—like you could do things better or more easily than usual, or that your ideas or abilities were especially great?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I honestly believed I could turn my little operation into the best boutique restoration shop around. I figured I could handle my wife's care and take on every single job at the same time without dropping a thing. I just felt completely capable of doing it all, no problem.\n\nDoctor: Did your wife or anyone on your crew ever comment that this burst of energy and confidence seemed to go on for about a week or more, rather than just a couple of days?\n\nPatient: No, nobody ever said anything like that. My wife hasn't really been in a position to notice... and the guys at the shop, they just knew I was on them about getting things done. If they thought it lasted a week or more, they didn't mention it to me. They just kind of kept their heads down and worked. So, I really don't know how long it seemed to them.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me approximately how long that energetic stretch lasted in relation to something memorable—like was it shorter than a weekend trip, or did it stretch across more than one full week, such as spanning from one weekend to the next?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. Like I said, I wasn't really tracking the days. It wasn't tied to a trip or a specific weekend or anything like that. It was just a block of time where I was going full tilt, but whether it was a few days or a full week... I just don't have a clear memory of how long it stretched out.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember whether that energetic stretch was still going on when you woke up on four separate mornings in a row—like did it carry over from one day to the next, to the next, to the next—or does it feel like it may have only been a day or two before you felt back to normal?\n\nPatient: I... I really couldn't tell you for sure. The days just kind of ran together. I'd wake up and get straight to work, but I wasn't keeping track of how many mornings in a row it was. \n\nIt might have been four days, or it might have just been two. I just can't give you a straight answer on that, because I honestly don't remember the exact day-to-day breakdown.\n\nDoctor: During that energetic stretch, did anyone close to you—like your wife before she got sick, or your crew—notice a distinct change in your behavior that seemed different from your usual self, even if it didn't cause problems?\n\nPatient: I don't think anyone really noticed a distinct change, no. Like I said, my wife hasn't been well enough to really pick up on things like that for a while. And the guys on the crew... they just know me as the boss. If I'm pushing them hard, they just figure I'm focused on a deadline or stressed about a job. I don't think they saw it as me being a different person or acting out of character. They just thought I was being demanding. So, no, nobody ever pulled me aside and said I was acting differently than usual.\n\nDoctor: Do you keep any work records, invoices, or job logs from that busy period—something that might show exactly how many days in a row you were taking on those extra restoration jobs?\n\nPatient: I don't really have anything like that. I mean, I keep track of invoices and receipts for materials, sure, but it's not like a daily timecard. I don't punch a clock. The paperwork just shows when a job was finished or when I bought lumber, not how many hours I was in the shop on a Tuesday versus a Wednesday. So, no, looking at the books wouldn't really narrow down the days for you.\n\nDoctor: Aside from this recent period, have you ever had other times in your life—months or years ago—where you felt unusually energetic, confident, or needed less sleep for several days, even before your wife became ill?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, I've always been a hard worker, and there are times when a big project comes up and I push through to get it done. But... nothing that stands out like what you're describing. I don't remember ever feeling unusually energetic or not needing sleep for days on end before all this recent stuff with my wife. Just normal busy periods, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember whether that energetic, fired-up stretch included more than one Sunday church gathering—that is, did it start before one Sunday service and still continue through the next one a week later?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you for sure. The days kind of just blurred together when I was working that hard. I know it lasted a good while, but I wasn't really tracking it by Sundays or church services. It might have crossed over two Sundays, or maybe it didn't. I just remember being constantly on the move, but I don't have a clear timeline in my head like that.\n\nDoctor: Besides losing interest in things you used to enjoy, have you also noticed changes in your sleep, appetite, energy, concentration, or any feelings of guilt or worthlessness over these past two months?\n\nPatient: Well, it's mostly just this... this heavy feeling. Most mornings I wake up and there's just this empty, heavy weight right in my chest. It's like all the color has just drained out of the house. I don't know about all those other things you mentioned, but I know I have to really force myself to hold it together so I don't just break down crying in front of my wife. It's just a really dark, heavy feeling every day.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two months, have you noticed changes in your appetite or weight, either eating much more or much less than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any big changes with my eating. I guess my appetite is about the same as it usually is. I haven't been eating a lot more or a lot less, and my weight seems pretty steady. It's really just that heavy feeling I mentioned that's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back to when your wife's health first declined—do you recall whether that fired-up, energetic stretch was over within the same week as that event, or did it continue on for more than a week afterward?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't say. When she got sick, everything was just chaos. I know I was running on high gear, trying to fix the house up and take care of things, but I honestly don't remember exactly how many days it went on after that happened. It's all just a blur from that time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
